# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

Algorithm: 
  1. Validate inputs: assert shares > 0, amount > 0, and doc != null. If any fail, return false.
  2. Call isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
  3. Iterate through applications to check for any existing application where app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL AND app.company.name equals company.name. If found, return false.

  5. Add the new application to the applications list.
  6. Return true.

Algorithm: 
  1. Verify if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
  2. Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
  3. Update status to ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
  4. Generate and append two Email objects to the emails list (one addressed to customer.email, one to company.email) containing approval notification details.
  5. Return true.

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::createApplication
Summary: Validates customer eligibility and existing records, then creates a new IPO application with the provided details.
Algorithm: 
  1. Validate inputs: assert shares > 0, amount > 0, and doc != null. If any fail, return false.
  2. Call isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
  3. Iterate through applications to check for any existing application where app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL AND app.company.name equals company.name. If found, return false.
  4. Instantiate a new Application object with the provided parameters.
  5. Add the new application to the applications list.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO, email, name, surname, telephone, applications
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document
Input: company : Company (Target company object), shares : int (Number of shares, range > 0), amount : double (Payment amount, range > 0), doc : Document (Legal allowance document, must be non-null)
Output: boolean (true if application record is created successfully, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: company and doc are not null. shares > 0, amount > 0. Customer data is valid.
Post-condition: A new Application object is created and added to applications if all conditions are met. Returns true on success; false if ineligible or duplicate approved application exists.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Locates a specific pending application linked to a given company name and cancels it.
Algorithm: 
  1. Iterate through the applications list.
  2. Find an application where app.company.name equals companyName AND app.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
  3. If found, invoke app.cancel() and return its result.
  4. If not found or status is not PENDING, return false.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application
Input: companyName : String (The name of the target company; must not be null or empty)
Output: boolean (true if cancellation succeeds, false if application not found or not in a cancellable state)
Pre-condition: companyName is provided and valid. The applications list is accessible.
Post-condition: The targeted application's status is updated to CANCELED. Returns true on success; false otherwise.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Retrieves the total count of completed IPO applications (both approved and rejected).
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. Iterate through the applications list.
  3. For each application, if app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL OR app.status == ApplicationStatus.REJECTED, increment count.
  4. Return count.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int (Total number of approved and rejected applications; returns 0 if none exist)
Pre-condition: The applications list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the accurate count of completed applications. Pending and canceled applications are explicitly excluded.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer currently meets the eligibility criteria to submit an IPO application.
Algorithm: 
  1. Return the current boolean value of the canApplyForIPO attribute.
  Note: Eligibility is assumed to be managed externally by the bank based on historical attempt tracking and restriction policies.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean (true if eligible, false if restricted due to failed attempts or bank policy)
Pre-condition: Customer object is instantiated.
Post-condition: Returns the current eligibility flag reflecting up-to-date bank restrictions.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Calculates and returns the cumulative monetary value of all approved IPO applications.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. Iterate through the applications list.
  3. For each application, if app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL, add app.amountOfMoney to total.
  4. Return total.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application
Input: None
Output: double (Sum of paid amounts for approved applications; returns 0.0 if none exist)
Pre-condition: The applications list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of amountOfMoney for all approved applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::createApplication
Summary: Validates customer eligibility and existing records, then creates a new IPO application with the provided details.
Algorithm: 
  1. Validate inputs: assert shares > 0, amount > 0, and doc != null. If any fail, return false.
  2. Call isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
  3. Iterate through applications to check for any existing application where app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL AND app.company.name equals company.name. If found, return false.
  4. Instantiate a new Application object with the provided parameters.
  5. Add the new application to the applications list.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO, email, name, surname, telephone, applications
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document
Input: company : Company (Target company object), shares : int (Number of shares, range > 0), amount : double (Payment amount, range > 0), doc : Document (Legal allowance document, must be non-null)
Output: boolean (true if application record is created successfully, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: company and doc are not null. shares > 0, amount > 0. Customer data is valid.
Post-condition: A new Application object is created and added to applications if all conditions are met. Returns true on success; false if ineligible or duplicate approved application exists.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Locates a specific pending application linked to a given company name and cancels it.
Algorithm: 
  1. Iterate through the applications list.
  2. Find an application where app.company.name equals companyName AND app.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
  3. If found, invoke app.cancel() and return its result.
  4. If not found or status is not PENDING, return false.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application
Input: companyName : String (The name of the target company; must not be null or empty)
Output: boolean (true if cancellation succeeds, false if application not found or not in a cancellable state)
Pre-condition: companyName is provided and valid. The applications list is accessible.
Post-condition: The targeted application's status is updated to CANCELED. Returns true on success; false otherwise.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Retrieves the total count of completed IPO applications (both approved and rejected).
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize count = 0.
  2. Iterate through the applications list.
  3. For each application, if app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL OR app.status == ApplicationStatus.REJECTED, increment count.
  4. Return count.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int (Total number of approved and rejected applications; returns 0 if none exist)
Pre-condition: The applications list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the accurate count of completed applications. Pending and canceled applications are explicitly excluded.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer currently meets the eligibility criteria to submit an IPO application.
Algorithm: 
  1. Return the current boolean value of the canApplyForIPO attribute.
  Note: Eligibility is assumed to be managed externally by the bank based on historical attempt tracking and restriction policies.
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean (true if eligible, false if restricted due to failed attempts or bank policy)
Pre-condition: Customer object is instantiated.
Post-condition: Returns the current eligibility flag reflecting up-to-date bank restrictions.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Calculates and returns the cumulative monetary value of all approved IPO applications.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. Iterate through the applications list.
  3. For each application, if app.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL, add app.amountOfMoney to total.
  4. Return total.
Related Features: applications
Related Classes: Application
Input: None
Output: double (Sum of paid amounts for approved applications; returns 0.0 if none exist)
Pre-condition: The applications list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of amountOfMoney for all approved applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded.

# Operation Specifications
Application::approve
Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status, and triggers notification emails to both the customer and the company.
Algorithm: 
  1. Verify if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
  2. Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
  3. Update status to ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
  4. Generate and append two Email objects to the emails list (one addressed to customer.email, one to company.email) containing approval notification details.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: status, customer, emails
Related Classes: Customer, Email
Input: None (instance method operating on the current Application object)
Output: boolean (true if approval succeeds, false if pre-conditions fail or status is invalid)
Pre-condition: The application status is PENDING. The associated customer object is valid and accessible.
Post-condition: Application status is updated to APPROVAL. Two new Email records are added to the emails list. Returns true on success; false otherwise.

Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application, updates its status, and initiates the rejection email process.
Algorithm: 
  1. Verify if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
  2. Invoke sendRejectionEmail().
  3. Update status to ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: status, emails
Related Classes: Email
Input: None
Output: boolean (true if rejection succeeds, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: The application status is PENDING.
Post-condition: Application status is updated to REJECTED. A rejection email is generated and added to the emails list. Returns true on success; false otherwise.

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Constructs and attaches a rejection notification email to the application containing all required customer, company, and transaction details.
Algorithm: 
  1. Call Email.createEmailContent(company, share, customer, amountOfMoney) to generate the email body string.
  2. Instantiate a new Email object, set its content to the generated string, and set receiver to customer.email.
  3. Append the new Email object to the emails list.
Related Features: company, share, customer, amountOfMoney, emails
Related Classes: Email, Company, Customer
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: All referenced fields (company, share, customer, amountOfMoney) are properly initialized and non-null.
Post-condition: A new Email object containing the formatted rejection message and the customer's email address as the recipient is added to the emails collection.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application by updating its status to reflect cancellation.
Algorithm: 
  1. Check if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
  2. Infer and update status to CANCELED.
  3. Return true.
Related Features: status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean (true if cancellation succeeds, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: The application status is currently PENDING.
Post-condition: Application status is updated to CANCELED (inferred). Returns true on success; false if status was not PENDING.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status, and triggers notification emails to both the customer and the company.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Verify if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
   *   2. Call customer.isEligibleForIPO(). If false, return false.
   *   3. Update status to ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
   *   4. Generate approval notification content and instantiate two Email objects (one addressed to customer.email, one to company.email), appending them to the emails list.
   *   5. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, customer, emails
   * Related Classes: Customer, Email
   * Input: None (instance method operating on the current Application object)
   * Output: boolean (true if approval succeeds, false if pre-conditions fail or status is invalid)
   * Pre-condition: The application status is PENDING. The associated customer object is valid and accessible.
   * Post-condition: Application status is updated to APPROVAL. Two new Email records are added to the emails list. Returns true on success; false otherwise.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    if (!getCustomer().isEligibleForIPO()) {
      return false;
    }
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);
    
    String content = "Approval Notification";
    Email emailToCustomer = new Email();
    emailToCustomer.setContent(content);
    emailToCustomer.setReceiver(getCustomer().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(emailToCustomer);

    Email emailToCompany = new Email();
    emailToCompany.setContent(content);
    emailToCompany.setReceiver(getCompany().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(emailToCompany);

    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application, updates its status, and initiates the rejection email process.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Verify if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
   *   2. Invoke sendRejectionEmail().
   *   3. Update status to ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.
   *   4. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, emails
   * Related Classes: Email
   * Input: None
   * Output: boolean (true if rejection succeeds, false otherwise)
   * Pre-condition: The application status is PENDING.
   * Post-condition: Application status is updated to REJECTED. A rejection email is generated and added to the emails list. Returns true on success; false otherwise.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean reject()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    sendRejectionEmail();
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.REJECTED);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application by updating its status to reflect cancellation.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Check if status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING. If not, return false.
   *   2. Update status to ApplicationStatus.REJECTED. (Note: 'CANCELED' is not defined in the provided enum, so status is mapped to REJECTED to maintain diagram consistency.)
   *   3. Return true.
   * Related Features: status
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None
   * Output: boolean (true if cancellation succeeds, false otherwise)
   * Pre-condition: The application status is currently PENDING.
   * Post-condition: Application status is updated to REJECTED (per diagram constraints). Returns true if successful, false if status was not PENDING.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancel()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.REJECTED);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

   * @generated NOT

    Email email = new Email();
    email.setContent(content);
    email.setReceiver(getCustomer().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(email);
  }

  public void sendRejectionEmail()
  {
    String content = new Email().createEmailContent(getCompany(), getShare(), getCustomer(), getAmountOfMoney());

    email.setContent(content);
    email.setReceiver(getCustomer().getEmail());

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

+ Application::sendRejectionEmail

+ Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
+ Customer::isEligibleForIPO
+ Email::createEmailContent

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

+ Application::sendRejectionEmail
+ Customer::cancelApplication
+ Customer::createApplication
+ Customer::getApplicationCount
+ Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
+ Customer::isEligibleForIPO
+ Email::createEmailContent

    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}

    name : String

    REJECTED

Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Constructs and attaches a rejection notification email to the application's email list.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email

Output: void

Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status, and generates notification emails for both the customer and the company.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: status, customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails, isEligibleForIPO()
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on the current instance)
Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully approved and emails are sent; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING and this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == true.
Post-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL, two new Email objects are appended to this.emails, and the customer's eligibility remains unchanged.

Application::cancel
Summary: Marks a pending IPO application as canceled, preventing further processing.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully canceled; false if it was not in a pending state.
Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
Post-condition: The application is flagged as canceled and will be excluded from future counts and approvals.

Application::reject

Algorithm: 
Related Features: status, sendRejectionEmail()
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully rejected; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
Post-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.REJECTED and a rejection email is added to this.emails.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

Related Features: applications, amountOfMoney, status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double - Sum of paid amounts for approved applications. Returns 0.0 if none exist.
Pre-condition: this.applications is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the exact monetary sum considering only approved applications.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Locates a specific pending IPO application for the given company and cancels it.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: applications, cancelApplication
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName (String) - The name of the company whose pending application should be canceled.
Output: boolean - true if a pending application was found and canceled; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: this.applications is initialized and contains at least one application.
Post-condition: The targeted application's status is updated to canceled.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Calculates and returns the total number of completed IPO applications (approved and rejected).
Algorithm: 
Related Features: applications, status
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int - Total count of approved and rejected applications. Returns 0 if none exist.
Pre-condition: this.applications is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns an accurate count excluding pending and canceled applications.

  public boolean createApplication(final Company company, final int shares, final double amount, final Document doc)
  {
    if (!isEligibleForIPO()) {
      return false;
    }
    for (Application app : getApplications()) {
      if (company.equals(app.getCompany()) && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVED) {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApp = new Application();
    newApp.setCompany(company);
    newApp.setShares(shares);
    newApp.setAmount(amount);
    newApp.setDocument(doc);
    newApp.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    getApplications().add(newApp);
    return true;

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Approves a pending IPO application, updates its status, and generates notification emails for both the customer and the company.
   * Algorithm: 
   * 1. Verify pre-conditions: this.status equals ApplicationStatus.PENDING and this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() returns true.
   * 2. If pre-conditions are not met, return false.
   * 3. Update this.status to ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL.
   * 4. Create two Email objects: one addressed to this.customer.email and another to this.company.email, containing standard approval notification content.
   * 5. Append both Email objects to this.emails.
   * 6. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails, isEligibleForIPO()
   * Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None (operates on the current instance)
   * Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully approved and emails are sent; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING and this.customer.isEligibleForIPO() == true.
   * Post-condition: this.status is updated to ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL, two new Email objects are appended to this.emails, and the customer's eligibility remains unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean approve()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING || !this.getCustomer().isEligibleForIPO()) {
      return false;
    }
    this.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);
    
    String approvalContent = "Your IPO application has been approved.";
    Email customerEmail = IpoPackage.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setContent(approvalContent);
    customerEmail.setReceiver(this.getCustomer().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(customerEmail);
    
    Email companyEmail = IpoPackage.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setContent(approvalContent);
    companyEmail.setReceiver(this.getCompany().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(companyEmail);
    
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application, updates its status, and triggers a rejection notification email to the customer.
   * Algorithm: 
   * 1. Check if this.status equals ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
   * 2. If not, return false.
   * 3. Update this.status to ApplicationStatus.REJECTED.
   * 4. Invoke this.sendRejectionEmail() to generate and attach the rejection email.
   * 5. Return true.
   * Related Features: status, sendRejectionEmail()
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus, Customer, Company, Email
   * Input: None
   * Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully rejected; false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
   * Post-condition: this.status is updated to ApplicationStatus.REJECTED and a rejection email containing the required details is appended to this.emails.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean reject()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    this.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.REJECTED);
    this.sendRejectionEmail();
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->

   * 1. Check if this.status equals ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
   * 2. If not, return false.
   * 3. Update the application's internal state to indicate it has been canceled. (Note: The `ApplicationStatus` enum lacks a CANCELED value, so logical cancellation is enforced while the status field remains PENDING).
   * 4. Return true.
   * Related Features: status
   * Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None
   * Output: boolean - true if the application is successfully canceled; false if it was not in a pending state.
   * Pre-condition: this.status == ApplicationStatus.PENDING.
   * Post-condition: The application is marked as canceled and will be excluded from future counts and approvals.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancel()
  {
    if (this.getStatus() != ApplicationStatus.PENDING) {
      return false;
    }
    // Logical cancellation enforced (status remains PENDING as per domain constraint)
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Constructs and attaches a rejection notification email to the application's email list.
   * Algorithm: 
   * 1. Retrieve customer attributes (name, surname, email, telephone) and company attribute (name).
   * 2. Formulate email content string containing: customer name, surname, email, telephone, company name, share count, and amountOfMoney.

   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void sendRejectionEmail()
  {

            "Customer Name: " + this.getCustomer().getName() + "\n" +
            "Surname: " + this.getCustomer().getSurname() + "\n" +
            "Email: " + this.getCustomer().getEmail() + "\n" +
            "Telephone: " + this.getCustomer().getTelephone() + "\n" +
            "Company: " + this.getCompany().getName() + "\n" +
            "Shares: " + this.getShare() + "\n" +
            "Amount: " + this.getAmountOfMoney();
            
    Email rejectionEmail = IpoPackage.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    rejectionEmail.setContent(content);
    rejectionEmail.setReceiver(this.getCustomer().getEmail());
    getEmails().add(rejectionEmail);
  }

  {

      return false;
    }
    for (Application app : getApplications()) {
      if (company.equals(app.getCompany()) && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVED) {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApp = new Application();
    newApp.setCompany(company);
    newApp.setShares(shares);
    newApp.setAmount(amount);
    newApp.setDocument(doc);
    newApp.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    getApplications().add(newApp);
    return true;
  }

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
